● "Unpin others" is being built. The record had no earlier decision on it, so
  the design is mine. Say if any part is not what you meant.

  - Where it lives. Editors solve the same thing with "Close Others" in a tab's
    menu: the action sits in the menu of the one that stays. So "Unpin others",
    in your words, becomes an item in the More menu of a file's card, a symbol
    row and a 3D sphere, and on each pin's entry in the pin tray.
  - When it shows. Only when at least one other pin exists. Otherwise it is
    absent, not greyed out.
  - What it does. It releases every other pin in one step. The thing you used it
    on stays as it was: a pinned file stays pinned, a symbol row keeps only
    that symbol's pin, and the subject stays the subject. A file that was on
    screen only because another pin showed it becomes the one pin, since it
    would otherwise vanish with the rest.
  - Undo. It is one step of the page's history, so the browser's Back restores
    everything released.
  - Focus. Keyboard focus stays on the thing you kept, and the page announces
    once how many were released.

  The history round's first part is on main. I read the code and looked at the
  pictures before landing it.

  - The pinned file stays lit. At your step 762
    (AI-Agent-Workspace/AgentSessionHistory/2026/11/2026-11-18/Screenshots/histo
    ry-pinned/02-after-step-762-pathfind-undimmed.png), pathfind.ts is now a
    whole bright marble among the dimmed spheres.
  - Coarse and fine buttons. ‹ › still move one commit. The new outer pair, a
    chevron against a bar, jumps to the next mark. Page Up and Page Down do the
    same.
  - Play follows the marks when something is pinned.
  - Your "major markers" question is moot for this file. The engine's
    pathfind.ts has 8 tall marks and no short ones, so both readings give the
    same stops.
  - Two older layout faults were fixed along the way. At your window width with
    the navigation open, the scrubber's track was squeezed to 146 px; it now
    gets its own row at 511 px. Below 850 px the control strip no longer covers
    the scrubber's buttons.

  It is not on your port until the gate rebuilds the Explorer, and the browser
  suite has not run over it yet. The builder ran the history cases on its own
  branch: 45 of 48 on the first run, then the mended cases passed, but it did
  not rerun two of the three history specs in full after its last layout fixes.

  Seven builders are out. The gate starts when the three midnight rounds hand
  back, or at 04:30 UTC over what has landed.

❯ >The record had no earlier decision on it, so the design is mine. Say if any
  part is not what you meant.

  True. If it helps (hopefully doesn't lead you astray), it is notable that
  inside IDEs, there is a difference between a file being "open" (eventually
  disappears in the stack of tabs once it is sufficiently temporally distant
  from the user's interactions) and "pinned" (durable to wiping the slate clean
  of tabs). It is also notable that we currently don't have much in the way of
  a "re-wrap with a bubble" interaction in 3D. Whether the two have any
  semantic equivalence is not yet clear to me, but I wonder if two design holes
  could be plugged semantically together by a common unifying user
  interaction.

● It doesn't lead me astray; it points at something the product half has
  already. I checked what exists.

  The Explorer already has your three editor states, unnamed.

  ┌────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      In an editor      │             In the Explorer today              │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Preview tab, replaced  │ The 3D map's "discovery": one slot, filled     │
  │ by the next file you   │ when you inspect something through More,       │
  │ look at                │ replaced by the next one                       │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Open tab, stays until  │ An opened directory, or in 3D a popped bubble  │
  │ closed                 │                                                │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Pinned tab, survives a │ A pin                                          │
  │  wipe                  │                                                │
  └────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Re-wrapping exists in 3D, but you couldn't find it. An opened bubble carries a
  small chip named "Wrap … again", and a directory's own sphere has Close. You
  use the map daily and didn't know, so in practice it is missing.

  The real hole is that nothing lets go. Since your request of November 2, a
  plain click on a 3D sphere pins it. Every step of an exploration is therefore
  durable, and the picture only grows.

  My recommendation for the unifying interaction: one gesture at two strengths,
  in the menu of the thing you keep. Not built.

  - Fold others away: everything merely open elsewhere goes back. Opened
    directories close, bubbles wrap again, the discovery is released. Pins stay.
    This is the editor's "Close Others", and in 3D the wrapping is what letting
    go looks like.
  - Unpin others: the same, and the other pins go too.

  I would not copy the other half of the editor's behaviour, where an open tab
  disappears by itself once it is old. A tab's position means nothing, so it can
  vanish unnoticed. On a map, a thing that leaves by itself costs you your
  bearings.

  What I changed in tonight's build. "Unpin others" is now defined as exactly
  what Clear all pins releases, minus the thing you used it on, so the two
  actions cannot drift apart. The builder will also list anything Clear all pins
  leaves open that you would count as "everything", such as an opened directory
  that isn't a pin, a popped bubble, or the discovery. That list is the
  evidence for whether "Fold others away" needs to be its own item.

  Do you want the two-strength version built once that list is in? I'd also make
  the "Wrap again" chip findable in the same round.

› Message from general-purpose
  history-pinned builder, milestone two (the track's zoom): I'm working on a new
  branch `history-zoom`, cut from history-pinned's head (58792127). Commit
  366e66e5 holds the code, its unit cases, the documents, and the browser cases,
  which are written but not yet run. Unit cases are green (76 across history/
  and the emphasis module). The client and the tests type-check and lint clean.
  I'm off the lane until you say the gate is done; one script then does the
  build, my eye, the new cases against main's build, the three history specs,
  and safe:commit.

  What it does:
  - **Wheel over the track or ruler:** zooms the range about the step under the
  pointer, by exp(delta), so a trackpad is smooth and wheel notches are even.
  - **Pinch:** Ctrl+wheel does the same, read more strongly. Two fingers on
  touch pinch about their middle and slide.
  - **Slide:** a sideways wheel, or Shift with the wheel, slides the range.
  - **Keys:** + and - on the focused slider zoom about the current step.
  - **Least span:** 8 steps, or a shorter history's whole. A finder range
  already narrower than 8 is never widened by zooming in.
  - **Marks sharpen:** `automaticGranularity` already counted the range's own
  steps (`rangedTickModel` slices the dates), so nothing needed mending there. A
  unit case now holds it: three years at ten commits a day resolve months →
  weeks → days → single commits.
  - **Step outside the range:** a zoom pauses nothing and can leave the current
  step outside the range. Then the thumb leaves the track and a mark in its look
  stands at that end. That mark is a 24 px button, "Show the current commit,
  <date>", which slides the range the least amount to hold the step.

  Rules I changed, and the ones I kept:
  - **Changed:** the fine pair, the arrows, the coarse pair and Page Up/Down no
  longer stop at the range's ends. They pass them and slide the range the least
  amount. They are disabled only at the history's ends. Beyond the range, the
  coarse marks are the whole history's.
  - **Kept:** direct seeking outside the range (finder, a file chosen at its
  moment, a link) still restores the whole range. Home and End still go to the
  range's ends. Play still stops at the range's end; it first slides the range
  to hold a step a zoom left outside.
  - **One existing case updated:** the range-selection case used to assert Next
  commit disabled at the range's end. It now asserts that Next passes the end
  and the range slides from [2,4] to [3,5].

  Not yet verified:
  - **Everything in a browser:** wheel, pinch, keys, the off-range mark, and
  play continuing during a zoom.
  - **The zoom on this repository by eye:** a five-frame strip with pathfind.ts
  pinned, the marks sharpening.
  - **The narrow layouts with the mark.**
  - **The new cases failing on main.**
  - **Two-finger touch:** no case covers it. Playwright's desktop Chrome has no
  multi-touch, so it will be verified by reading only.
  - **Live Docs regeneration:** zoom.ts and its test have authored Purpose and
  Notes. Their generated regions wait for the generator, which runs inside the
  gate. Those Notes cite #turn-26 of the Nov 18 record, which exists on main but
  not on my branch. If the gate's link check reads anchors, it may fail on my
  branch only.

  I'll wait for your word. If none comes by 05:10 I'll leave it at this commit,
  as you asked.

  others":

  - Fold others away: other opened directories close, their 3D bubbles wrap
    again, and the discovery is released. Pins stay.

❯ Once "History follows the pinned set" builder's work is done, I think we
  should get serious about ensuring that the 2D Local Map has the ability to
  explore history as well. We should be able to scrub through history in the
  same shared control whose state is continuous in 2D and 3D local map. (Future
  idea: pin a date in the World Map and then peek into the temporally-matched
  state of all temporally-scanned objects when looking inside them). How might
  you design such a thing?

  In addition, I want to hear about design ideas for how we eventually ditch
  the Detail Panel for good (if possible; otherwise for extraordinarily rare
  sparing use), and possibly even the Left Sidbar? How do we ensure that we're
  making great use of our screen real estate and making sure that our software
  shuts up and the scanned materials do they talking? How do we best get out of
  the way so users on many different viewport sizes and dimensions can still
  understand complex directories of things with simple idiomatic gestures and
  intuitive controls? (idea for later: first-class support for generic gamepads
  as input to Live Documentation Explorer as a means of ensuring that our
  design is simple enough that it _could_ easily be navigated via a
  controller).

● Here is how I'd design both. Nothing below is built; I read the history plan
  and the earlier decisions on the reader and sidebar first.

  History in the 2D map

  The obstacle. The history file carries each file's shape at every commit
  (kind, symbol count, wires between files). It does not carry symbol names or
  symbol-to-symbol links, so a 2D card's rows cannot be drawn truthfully for a
  past commit. That is why the October 16 plan left 2D out.

  The moment belongs to the app, not to the 3D map. One scrubber, in one place,
  for both maps. Switching 2D ↔ 3D keeps the commit you are on. Today that
  switch snaps back to the present and closes the control.

  Three rungs, each showing only what it knows:

  Rung: 1
  What 2D shows at a past commit: Files, directories, one line per pair of
    files with its count, and "+N symbols" on each card. This is the Flow look
    zoomed out.
  What it needs: Nothing new. It is exactly what the history already carries.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Rung: 2
  What 2D shows at a past commit: Symbol rows and their wires as they were,
    with changed rows lit.
  What it needs: The history must also carry symbols and their links per
    commit. The file grows and is rebuilt once.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Rung: 3
  What 2D shows at a past commit: The descriptions as written that day.
  What it needs: Text per commit. I'd skip this and show today's words, marked
    as today's, as the reader already does.

  Keeping your bearings while scrubbing. While the thumb drags, cards hold their
  places and only light up, appear or fade. When it rests, the layout
  re-settles with the move animation 2D already has. 3D follows the same rule:
  cheap while dragging, settle at rest.

  The World Map date. Across several repositories a commit means nothing, so the
  moment should be a date. Each scanned thing resolves it to its own latest
  commit on or before that date, and a thing without history says so.

  I'd build rung 1 next. It needs no format change and will show whether
  file-level 2D history is already useful. I'd measure rung 2's file size before
  committing to it.

  Retiring the Detail Panel

  Each thing you open it for gets a home on the map:

  You open it for: Purpose and symbol notes
  New home: Already on the card.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  You open it for: The lists of what uses a file and what it uses
  New home: The search field: "what uses X" becomes a query and the results
    are the list. That leaves one list in the whole app. Tonight's two
    smallest playtesters found their answers in that list, so it has value; it
    just shouldn't need a panel.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  You open it for: Long notes and documents
  New home: The card's closest zoom. Past reading size, a card becomes its own
    page, full-screen on a phone. Zooming out returns you.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  You open it for: What a commit changed
  New home: The step panel, which exists.

  That leaves the panel empty. I'd keep a dialog only for overflow, which you
  allowed on November 3.

  Retiring the sidebar

  It is 260 px wide, nearly a third of your 834 px window.

  ┌───────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Sidebar item    │                      New home                       │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Knowledge Sources │ A breadcrumb beside search: World › payments › Data │
  │  / World Map /    │  › PaymentsContext.cs. Each crumb steps out one     │
  │ Local Map         │ scale. You suggested this on September 30.          │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Show Tests, Show  │ The legend's own rows. Click a kind in the key to   │
  │ Assets            │ hide it, so legend and filter become one object.    │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Legend            │ A chip on the bottom strip, which already has its   │
  │                   │ icon.                                               │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Tuning            │ A sheet opened from the strip's sliders icon.       │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ File counts, the  │ The root crumb's hover.                             │
  │ Context line      │                                                     │
  └───────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  wide                                  phone
  ┌──────────────────────────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐
  │ World › payments › Data   [find] │  │ ‹ Data [find] │
  │                                  │  │               │
  │            the map               │  │    the map    │
  │                                  │  │               │
  │ [fit][key][tune][2D|3D][when] 📌 │  │ [⋯][2D|3D] 📌 │
  └──────────────────────────────────┘  └───────────────┘

  One rule, six verbs, and the gamepad

  The rule. Words of ours appear only on a rested hover, in a menu, or in a
  sheet you opened. Everything always on screen is either the scan's own words
  or an icon.

  The verbs. Closer / further (zoom), follow (a wire), keep / let go (pin,
  fold), when (the moment), find, and back. Each gets one gesture per input
  device.

  The gamepad as a test. Mapping those six onto a controller is easy for five of
  them: triggers zoom, one button keeps, one goes back, bumpers step through
  time. What has no home is moving focus by direction, to the nearest thing
  left, right, up or down. The app has no such thing today; keyboard users Tab
  through everything. I'd build that first, for arrow keys. It helps keyboard
  users immediately and is the piece a controller would stand on.

  Two questions

  1. Should 2D history rung 1 go next, once the timeline zoom is verified?
  2. For the frame, shall I build the sidebar-free version behind a lever, with
     the sidebar still available, so you can judge it in the app rather than
     from these words?
